					<div class="vector-body-before-content">
							<div class="mw-indicators">
		</div>

						<div id="siteSub" class="noprint">From Wikipedia, the free encyclopedia</div>

					<div id="contentSub"><div id="mw-content-subtitle"></div></div>
					
					
					<div id="mw-content-text" class="mw-body-content"><div class="mw-subjectpageheader">
</div><div class="mw-content-ltr mw-parser-output" lang="en" dir="ltr"><p class="mw-empty-elt">
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1320445320">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}@media print{body.ns-0 .mw-parser-output .hatnote{display:none!important}}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">For keynote presentations given by Steve Jobs, see <a href="/wiki/Stevenote" title="Stevenote">Stevenote</a>.</div>
<p class="mw-empty-elt">
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1305433154">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output .ambox{margin:0 10%}}@media print{body.ns-0 .mw-parser-output .ambox{display:none!important}}</style><table class="plainlinks metadata ambox mbox-small-left ambox-notice" role="presentation" style="width: auto;"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><span typeof="mw:File"><span><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/1/1d/Information_icon4.svg/20px-Information_icon4.svg.png" decoding="async" width="20" height="20" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/1/1d/Information_icon4.svg/40px-Information_icon4.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="620" data-file-height="620" /></span></span></td><td class="mbox-text" style="width: auto;"><div class="mbox-text-span">This list is <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:WikiProject_Lists#Incomplete_lists" title="Wikipedia:WikiProject Lists">incomplete</a>; you can help by <a class="external text" data-mw-original-href="//en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit">adding missing items</a>.  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">August 2022</span>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:YBCA_iPad_2_preparation.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/YBCA_iPad_2_preparation.jpg/250px-YBCA_iPad_2_preparation.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="206" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/YBCA_iPad_2_preparation.jpg/500px-YBCA_iPad_2_preparation.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3744" data-file-height="3856" /></a><figcaption>The Yerba Buena Center for the Arts's exterior prepared in anticipation for the March 2, 2011 unveiling of Apple's <a href="/wiki/IPad_2" title="IPad 2">iPad 2</a></figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Apple_Inc." title="Apple Inc.">Apple Inc.</a> has announced major new and redesigned products and upgrades through press conferences, while minor updates often happen through press releases on Apple Newsroom. The press conferences have historically garnered a significant following in traditional and online media.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-3kinds&#95;1-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3kinds-1"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>1<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> The detailed agenda of the event is often kept as a secret to create <a href="/wiki/Marketing_buzz" title="Marketing buzz">buzz</a>, and only unveiled during the event, though event taglines sometimes give hints.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-CNN&#95;secrets&#95;2-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CNN_secrets-2"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>2<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> These events are usually streamed live on Apple's website and, in recent years, YouTube channel. Video replays of most Apple events since 2007 are available on Apple's "Apple Events" podcast.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-apple-events-podcast&#95;3-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-apple-events-podcast-3"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>3<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Apple has often announced new products at the annual <a href="/wiki/Apple_Worldwide_Developers_Conference" class="mw-redirect" title="Apple Worldwide Developers Conference">Worldwide Developers Conference</a> (WWDC), despite it being mainly software-focused.
</p><p>Apple has held events at the following venues:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Moscone_West" class="mw-redirect" title="Moscone West">Moscone West</a>, <a href="/wiki/San_Francisco" title="San Francisco">San Francisco</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Yerba_Buena_Center_for_the_Arts" title="Yerba Buena Center for the Arts">Yerba Buena Center for the Arts</a>, <a href="/wiki/San_Francisco" title="San Francisco">San Francisco</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Bill_Graham_Civic_Auditorium" title="Bill Graham Civic Auditorium">Bill Graham Civic Auditorium</a>, <a href="/wiki/San_Francisco" title="San Francisco">San Francisco</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Flint_Center" class="mw-redirect" title="Flint Center">Flint Center</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cupertino" class="mw-redirect" title="Cupertino">Cupertino</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/San_Jose_Convention_Center" title="San Jose Convention Center">McEnery Convention Center</a>, <a href="/wiki/San_Jose,_California" title="San Jose, California">San Jose</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Steve_Jobs_Theater" title="Steve Jobs Theater">Steve Jobs Theater</a> (<a href="/wiki/Apple_Park" title="Apple Park">Apple Park</a>), <a href="/wiki/Cupertino" class="mw-redirect" title="Cupertino">Cupertino</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Brooklyn_Academy_of_Music" title="Brooklyn Academy of Music">Brooklyn Academy of Music</a>, <a href="/wiki/New_York_City" title="New York City">New York City</a></li>
<li>Online-only (2020–March 2022, due to the <a href="/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic" title="COVID-19 pandemic">COVID-19 pandemic</a>)</li>
<li>Both online and in-person (June 2022–present)</li></ul>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="1984">1984</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: 1984"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Macintosh_event_(January_24,_1984)"><span id="Macintosh_event_.28January_24.2C_1984.29"></span>Macintosh event (January 24, 1984)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Macintosh event (January 24, 1984)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>The Macintosh was shown to shareholders at the <a href="/wiki/Flint_Center" class="mw-redirect" title="Flint Center">Flint Center</a> in Cupertino.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-macboston&#95;4-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-macboston-4"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>4<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>5<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Macintosh_event_(Boston_Computer_Society)"><span id="Macintosh_event_.28Boston_Computer_Society.29"></span>Macintosh event (Boston Computer Society)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Macintosh event (Boston Computer Society)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>The Macintosh was shown for a second time less than a week after the first event.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-macboston&#95;4-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-macboston-4"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>4<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Apple_II_Forever_event_(April_1984)"><span id="Apple_II_Forever_event_.28April_1984.29"></span>Apple II Forever event (April 1984)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Apple II Forever event (April 1984)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>The Apple II Forever Event was held in April 1984. Apple introduced the <a href="/wiki/Apple_IIc" title="Apple IIc">Apple IIc</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>6<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="1990">1990</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: 1990"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="WWDC_1990_(May_1990)"><span id="WWDC_1990_.28May_1990.29"></span>WWDC 1990 (May 1990)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: WWDC 1990 (May 1990)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Apple's 1st annual Worldwide Developers Conference was held from May 7 to 11, 1990.
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="1991">1991</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: 1991"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="WWDC_1991_(May_1991)"><span id="WWDC_1991_.28May_1991.29"></span>WWDC 1991 (May 1991)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: WWDC 1991 (May 1991)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Apple's 2nd annual Worldwide Developers Conference was held from May 13 to 17, 1991. At the conference, Apple announced the first public demonstration of the <a href="/wiki/QuickTime" title="QuickTime">QuickTime</a> video player.
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="1992">1992</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: 1992"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="WWDC_1992_(May_1992)"><span id="WWDC_1992_.28May_1992.29"></span>WWDC 1992 (May 1992)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: WWDC 1992 (May 1992)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Apple's 3rd annual Worldwide Developers Conference was held from May 11 to 15, 1992.
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="1993">1993</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: 1993"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="WWDC_1993_(May_1993)"><span id="WWDC_1993_.28May_1993.29"></span>WWDC 1993 (May 1993)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: WWDC 1993 (May 1993)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Apple's 4th annual Worldwide Developers Conference was held from May 10 to 14, 1993.
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="1994">1994</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: 1994"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="WWDC_1994_(May_1994)"><span id="WWDC_1994_.28May_1994.29"></span>WWDC 1994 (May 1994)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: WWDC 1994 (May 1994)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Apple's 5th annual Worldwide Developers Conference was held from May 15 to 20, 1994.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>7<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="1995">1995</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: 1995"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="WWDC_1995_(May_1995)"><span id="WWDC_1995_.28May_1995.29"></span>WWDC 1995 (May 1995)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: WWDC 1995 (May 1995)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Apple's 6th annual Worldwide Developers Conference was held from May 8 to 12, 1995.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>8<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> WWDC'95 almost mainly focused on <a href="/wiki/Copland_(operating_system)" title="Copland (operating system)">Copland</a>, a new operating system meant to replace the <a href="/wiki/Classic_Mac_OS" title="Classic Mac OS">classic Mac OS</a>. The project was never released for the general public due to stability issues and delayed caused by <a href="/wiki/Feature_creep" title="Feature creep">feature creep</a>.
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="1996">1996</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: 1996"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="WWDC_1996_(May_1996)"><span id="WWDC_1996_.28May_1996.29"></span>WWDC 1996 (May 1996)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: WWDC 1996 (May 1996)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Apple's 7th annual Worldwide Developers Conference was held from May 13 to 17, 1996.
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="1997">1997</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: 1997"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="MacWorld_Expo_and_NeXT_technology_announcement_(January_7,_1997)"><span id="MacWorld_Expo_and_NeXT_technology_announcement_.28January_7.2C_1997.29"></span>MacWorld Expo and NeXT technology announcement (January 7, 1997)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: MacWorld Expo and NeXT technology announcement (January 7, 1997)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Gil Amelio announced that Apple would be integrating operating system technology from <a href="/wiki/NeXT_Software" class="mw-redirect" title="NeXT Software">NeXT Software</a> into future Apple operating systems. (Apple had purchased NeXT a few weeks beforehand.) Steve Jobs spoke about the technology NeXT was bringing to Apple.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>9<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="WWDC_1997">WWDC 1997</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: WWDC 1997"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Apple's 8th annual Worldwide Developers Conference was held from May 12 to 16, 1997.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>10<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>11<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="1998">1998</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: 1998"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Seybold_Seminars_New_York_(1998)"><span id="Seybold_Seminars_New_York_.281998.29"></span>Seybold Seminars New York (1998)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Seybold Seminars New York (1998)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Steve Jobs introduced the first <a href="/wiki/Apple_Studio_Display_(1998%E2%80%932004)" title="Apple Studio Display (1998–2004)">Apple Studio Display</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-macworld9806&#95;12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-macworld9806-12"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>12<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> It was Apple's first desktop monitor to use LCD technology instead of CRT technology.
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Apple_event_(May_1998)"><span id="Apple_event_.28May_1998.29"></span>Apple event (May 1998)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Apple event (May 1998)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Steve Jobs introduced the <a href="/wiki/IMac_G3" title="IMac G3">original iMac</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-iMacMay1998&#95;13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-iMacMay1998-13"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>13<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> The event was held at the Flint Center.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-iMacMay1998&#95;13-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-iMacMay1998-13"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>13<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="WWDC_1998_(May_11-15,_1998)"><span id="WWDC_1998_.28May_11-15.2C_1998.29"></span>WWDC 1998 (May 11-15, 1998)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: WWDC 1998 (May 11-15, 1998)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Apple's 9th annual Worldwide Developers Conference was held at the San Jose Convention Center.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>14<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>In an unusual move, Apple announced the first iMac just days before WWDC. During the keynote, interim CEO Steve Jobs briefly recapped the iMac announcement before focusing on technical topics, primarily related to QuickTime and the transition strategy from the classic Mac OS to what would become Mac OS X.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>15<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="1999">1999</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: 1999"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Macworld_San_Francisco_(January_5,_1999)"><span id="Macworld_San_Francisco_.28January_5.2C_1999.29"></span>Macworld San Francisco (January 5, 1999)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Macworld San Francisco (January 5, 1999)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Macworld San Francisco was held on January 5, 1999. During the keynote on January 5, Steve Jobs presented the newly redesigned Power Macintosh G3, and previewed Mac OS X Server.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Internet&#95;Archive&#95;16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Internet_Archive-16"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>16<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="WWDC_1999_(May_10-14,_1999)"><span id="WWDC_1999_.28May_10-14.2C_1999.29"></span>WWDC 1999 (May 10-14, 1999)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: WWDC 1999 (May 10-14, 1999)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>WWDC 1999 was held at the San Jose Convention Center.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>17<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Macworld_Expo_NY_'99_(July_21,_1999)"><span id="Macworld_Expo_NY_.2799_.28July_21.2C_1999.29"></span>Macworld Expo NY '99 (July 21, 1999)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Macworld Expo NY &#039;99 (July 21, 1999)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Macworld New York 1999 was held on July 21, 1999.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-umeedu&#95;18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-umeedu-18"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>18<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Apple launched the <a href="/wiki/IBook" title="IBook">iBook</a> with optional wireless networking.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>19<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>20<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Seybold_(1999)"><span id="Seybold_.281999.29"></span>Seybold (1999)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Seybold (1999)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Seybold 1999 was held in the late summer.
</p><p>Steve Jobs announced the PowerPC G4 chip, the <a href="/wiki/Power_Mac_G4" title="Power Mac G4">Power Mac G4</a> and the first <a href="/wiki/Apple_Cinema_Display" title="Apple Cinema Display">Apple Cinema Display</a>, a 22-inch LCD.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>21<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>22<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>23<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Apple_special_event_(October_5,_1999)"><span id="Apple_special_event_.28October_5.2C_1999.29"></span>Apple special event (October 5, 1999)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Apple special event (October 5, 1999)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>The event was held at the <a href="/wiki/Flint_Center" class="mw-redirect" title="Flint Center">Flint Center</a>. <a href="/wiki/Mac_OS_9" title="Mac OS 9">Mac OS 9</a> was shown, the <a href="/wiki/IMac_DV" class="mw-redirect" title="IMac DV">iMac DV</a> was unveiled, and <a href="/wiki/IMovie" title="IMovie">iMovie</a> was introduced.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>24<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>25<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> A significant proportion of the audience was made up of Apple employees.
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="2000">2000</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: 2000"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Macworld_2000_(San_Francisco,_January_5,_2000)"><span id="Macworld_2000_.28San_Francisco.2C_January_5.2C_2000.29"></span>Macworld 2000 (San Francisco, January 5, 2000)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Macworld 2000 (San Francisco, January 5, 2000)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Macworld 2000 was held on January 5, 2000, in San Francisco,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>26<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> and featured the unveiling of the new <a href="/wiki/Aqua_(user_interface)" title="Aqua (user interface)">Aqua</a> user interface and <a href="/wiki/Quartz_(graphics_layer)" title="Quartz (graphics layer)">Quartz</a> graphics engine of the upcoming <a href="/wiki/Mac_OS_X" class="mw-redirect" title="Mac OS X">Mac OS X</a>, and Jobs' announcement that the <a href="/wiki/Mac_OS_X_10.0" title="Mac OS X 10.0">first major release of OS X</a> would ship in January 2001 after a <a href="/wiki/Mac_OS_X_Public_Beta" title="Mac OS X Public Beta">final preview release</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>27<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>28<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> (The actual release of Mac OS X happened in March 2001.) Jobs also announced that he had been promoted to full-time CEO of Apple.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>29<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="WWDC_2000_(May_15-19,_2000)"><span id="WWDC_2000_.28May_15-19.2C_2000.29"></span>WWDC 2000 (May 15-19, 2000)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: WWDC 2000 (May 15-19, 2000)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>The 11th annual WWDC was held at the San Jose Convention Center in San Jose, California.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>30<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Macworld_Expo,_New_York_(July_19,_2000)"><span id="Macworld_Expo.2C_New_York_.28July_19.2C_2000.29"></span>Macworld Expo, New York (July 19, 2000)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=35" title="Edit section: Macworld Expo, New York (July 19, 2000)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1305433154" /><table class="box-More&#95;citations&#95;needed&#95;section plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Refimprove" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="icon" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/60px-Question_book-new.svg.png" decoding="async" width="50" height="39" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/120px-Question_book-new.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="399" /></a></span></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section <b>needs additional citations for <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">verification</a></b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help <a href="/wiki/Special:EditPage/List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events" title="Special:EditPage/List of Apple Inc. media events">improve this article</a> by <a href="/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners" title="Help:Referencing for beginners">adding citations to reliable sources</a>&#32;in this section. Unsourced material may be challenged and removed.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">December 2024</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>On July 19, 2000, at the Macworld Expo New York held at the Jacob K. Javits Convention Center, Steve Jobs delivered a keynote introducing several new products.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>31<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Key announcements included the iMac DV Special Edition, the <a href="/wiki/Power_Mac_G4_Cube" title="Power Mac G4 Cube">Power Mac G4 Cube</a>, three new display models, and a full-surface optical mouse, which was gifted to all attendees. Apple also launched new displays, including a new version of the 22-inch Cinema Display that had an <a href="/wiki/Apple_Display_Connector" title="Apple Display Connector">Apple Display Connector (ADC)</a> connector.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>32<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="2001">2001</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=36" title="Edit section: 2001"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Macworld_2001_(San_Francisco)"><span id="Macworld_2001_.28San_Francisco.29"></span>Macworld 2001 (San Francisco)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=37" title="Edit section: Macworld 2001 (San Francisco)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<ul><li>The <a href="/wiki/PowerBook_G4" title="PowerBook G4">PowerBook G4</a> was announced, featuring a titanium design.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>33<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>34<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup></li>
<li>Updates to Mac OS X were shown.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>35<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup></li></ul>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="2002">2002</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=38" title="Edit section: 2002"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Macworld_2002_(San_Francisco,_January_7,_2002)"><span id="Macworld_2002_.28San_Francisco.2C_January_7.2C_2002.29"></span>Macworld 2002 (San Francisco, January 7, 2002)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=39" title="Edit section: Macworld 2002 (San Francisco, January 7, 2002)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Steve Jobs announced:
</p>
<ul><li>The <a href="/wiki/IMac_G4" title="IMac G4">iMac G4</a>, featuring an LCD for the first time in an iMac.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>36<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-lounge&#95;37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-lounge-37"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>37<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>38<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup></li>
<li>A new iBook with a 14" screen for $1799, alongside price and spec changes in the existing 12-inch models.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-lounge&#95;37-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-lounge-37"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>37<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>39<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/IPhoto" title="IPhoto">iPhoto</a><sup id="cite&#95;ref-lounge&#95;37-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-lounge-37"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>37<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup></li></ul>
<p>During the keynote, there was a demonstration of new versions of Adobe software including <a href="/wiki/Adobe_Illustrator" title="Adobe Illustrator">Adobe Illustrator</a> and <a href="/wiki/Adobe_Photoshop" title="Adobe Photoshop">Adobe Photoshop</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>40<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>The keynote was announced to be streamed live over the internet via QuickTime.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>41<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>42<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>The iMac G4 was leaked the night before the event from <a href="/wiki/Time_Magazine" class="mw-redirect" title="Time Magazine">Time Magazine</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>43<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Macworld_2002_New_York_(July_17,_2002)"><span id="Macworld_2002_New_York_.28July_17.2C_2002.29"></span>Macworld 2002 New York (July 17, 2002)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=40" title="Edit section: Macworld 2002 New York (July 17, 2002)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Steve Jobs introduced:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Mac_OS_X_10.2" class="mw-redirect" title="Mac OS X 10.2">Mac OS X 10.2</a> Jaguar, including <a href="/wiki/ICal" class="mw-redirect" title="ICal">iCal</a>, <a href="/wiki/ISync" title="ISync">iSync</a>, <a href="/wiki/.Mac" class="mw-redirect" title=".Mac">.Mac</a>, and iTunes 3.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-MacworldNY2002&#95;44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MacworldNY2002-44"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>44<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Disputed_statement" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:Disputed statement"><span title="This claim has reliable sources with contradicting facts (August 2023)">disputed</span></a>&#32;&#8211; <a href="/wiki/Talk:List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events#Jaguar_release_date" title="Talk:List of Apple Inc. media events">discuss</a></i>&#93;</sup></li>
<li>New iPods at lower prices, both 5GB and 10GB models, with the 10GB model having a solid state click wheel. A 20GB model was also introduced. Versions of the iPods for Windows were also announced.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-MacworldNY2002&#95;44-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MacworldNY2002-44"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>44<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Disputed_statement" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:Disputed statement"><span title="This claim has reliable sources with contradicting facts (August 2023)">disputed</span></a>&#32;&#8211; <a href="/wiki/Talk:List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events#iPods" title="Talk:List of Apple Inc. media events">discuss</a></i>&#93;</sup></li>
<li>A new version of the iMac G4 with a 17-inch screen.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-MacworldNY2002&#95;44-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MacworldNY2002-44"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>44<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup></li></ul>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Apple_Expo_2002,_Paris_(September_10,_2002)"><span id="Apple_Expo_2002.2C_Paris_.28September_10.2C_2002.29"></span>Apple Expo 2002, Paris (September 10, 2002)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=41" title="Edit section: Apple Expo 2002, Paris (September 10, 2002)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Much of the keynote consisted of a demo of Apple's new Mac OS X v10.2 operating system. It was announced that as of January 2003 all new Mac models would boot only into Mac OS X.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>45<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Steve Jobs also overviewed iCal and iSync and announced the availability of iCal on that day.
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="2003">2003</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=42" title="Edit section: 2003"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Macworld_2003_(San_Francisco,_January_20,_2003)"><span id="Macworld_2003_.28San_Francisco.2C_January_20.2C_2003.29"></span>Macworld 2003 (San Francisco, January 20, 2003)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=43" title="Edit section: Macworld 2003 (San Francisco, January 20, 2003)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Steve Jobs demoed iLife and introduced the 17-inch <a href="/wiki/PowerBook_G4" title="PowerBook G4">PowerBook G4</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>46<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="WWDC_2003_(June_23,_2003)"><span id="WWDC_2003_.28June_23.2C_2003.29"></span>WWDC 2003 (June 23, 2003)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=44" title="Edit section: WWDC 2003 (June 23, 2003)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Steve Jobs introduces <a href="/wiki/Xcode" title="Xcode">Xcode</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Power_Mac_G5" title="Power Mac G5">Power Mac G5</a>,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>47<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Mac_OS_X_Panther" title="Mac OS X Panther">Mac OS X 10.3 Panther</a>.
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Apple_Expo_2003,_Paris_(September_16,_2003)"><span id="Apple_Expo_2003.2C_Paris_.28September_16.2C_2003.29"></span>Apple Expo 2003, Paris (September 16, 2003)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=45" title="Edit section: Apple Expo 2003, Paris (September 16, 2003)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Steve Jobs announced:
</p>
<ul><li>An updated 17-inch and 12-inch <a href="/wiki/PowerBook_G4" title="PowerBook G4">PowerBook G4</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-AEP2003&#95;48-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AEP2003-48"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>48<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup></li>
<li>The first Aluminum 15-inch <a href="/wiki/PowerBook_G4" title="PowerBook G4">PowerBook G4</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-AEP2003&#95;48-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AEP2003-48"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>48<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>49<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>50<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup></li>
<li>A new wireless keyboard and mouse.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-AEP2003&#95;48-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AEP2003-48"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>48<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup></li></ul>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="2004">2004</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=46" title="Edit section: 2004"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Macworld_2004_(January_6-9,_2004)"><span id="Macworld_2004_.28January_6-9.2C_2004.29"></span>Macworld 2004 (January 6-9, 2004)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=47" title="Edit section: Macworld 2004 (January 6-9, 2004)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Macworld 2004 was held at the <a href="/wiki/Moscone_Center" title="Moscone Center">Moscone Center</a> in San Francisco. Steve Jobs introduced the <a href="/wiki/Xserve" title="Xserve">Xserve</a> G5, <a href="/wiki/Xserve" title="Xserve">Xserve</a> G5 RAID, and <a href="/wiki/IPod_Mini" title="IPod Mini">iPod Mini</a>. The 10GB <a href="/wiki/IPod_Classic" title="IPod Classic">iPod</a> also had its storage increased to 15GB for the same price of $299.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>51<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Apple_music_event,_London_(June_15,_2004)"><span id="Apple_music_event.2C_London_.28June_15.2C_2004.29"></span>Apple music event, London (June 15, 2004)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=48" title="Edit section: Apple music event, London (June 15, 2004)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Steve Jobs announced that the <a href="/wiki/ITunes_Music_Store" class="mw-redirect" title="ITunes Music Store">iTunes Music Store</a> would expand to Europe.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>52<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="WWDC_2004_(June_28,_2004)"><span id="WWDC_2004_.28June_28.2C_2004.29"></span>WWDC 2004 (June 28, 2004)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=49" title="Edit section: WWDC 2004 (June 28, 2004)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>New <a href="/wiki/Apple_Cinema_Display" title="Apple Cinema Display">Cinema Displays</a> with DVI connectors were announced. <a href="/wiki/Mac_OS_X_Tiger" title="Mac OS X Tiger">Mac OS X Tiger</a> was also announced.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>53<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Apple_music_event_(October_26,_2004)"><span id="Apple_music_event_.28October_26.2C_2004.29"></span>Apple music event (October 26, 2004)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=50" title="Edit section: Apple music event (October 26, 2004)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Steve Jobs introduced the <a href="/wiki/IPod_Photo" class="mw-redirect" title="IPod Photo">iPod Photo</a> line and a <a href="/wiki/U2" title="U2">U2</a> special edition of the iPod.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>54<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="2005">2005</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=51" title="Edit section: 2005"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Macworld_2005_(January_17,_2005,_San_Francisco)"><span id="Macworld_2005_.28January_17.2C_2005.2C_San_Francisco.29"></span>Macworld 2005 (January 17, 2005, San Francisco)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=52" title="Edit section: Macworld 2005 (January 17, 2005, San Francisco)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/IPod_Shuffle" title="IPod Shuffle">iPod Shuffle</a> was announced.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>55<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Mac_Mini" title="Mac Mini">Mac Mini</a>, iLife '05, and <a href="/wiki/IWork" title="IWork">iWork</a> were announced.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>56<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Steve Jobs announced that Apple sold more than 4.5 million iPods in the 2004 holiday quarter, compared to 733,000 iPods in the 2003 Holiday quarter.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>57<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>At the event, Steve Jobs referred to 2005 as the "year of HD video editing."<sup id="cite&#95;ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>58<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="WWDC_2005_(June_6–10,_2005)"><span id="WWDC_2005_.28June_6.E2.80.9310.2C_2005.29"></span>WWDC 2005 (June 6–10, 2005)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=53" title="Edit section: WWDC 2005 (June 6–10, 2005)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>After a basic market update, Jobs announced that Apple would transition the Macintosh platform to <a href="/wiki/Intel" title="Intel">Intel</a> <a href="/wiki/X86" title="X86">x86</a> processors after using <a href="/wiki/PowerPC" title="PowerPC">PowerPC</a> for more than a decade. The keynote featured developers from <a href="/wiki/Wolfram_Research" title="Wolfram Research">Wolfram Research</a>, who discussed their experience porting <a href="/wiki/Mathematica" class="mw-redirect" title="Mathematica">Mathematica</a> to <a href="/wiki/Mac_OS_X" class="mw-redirect" title="Mac OS X">Mac OS X</a> on the Intel platform. The conference consisted of 110 lab sessions and 95 presentation sessions, while more than 500 Apple engineers were on site alongside 3,800 attendees from 45 countries. The band <a href="/wiki/The_Wallflowers" title="The Wallflowers">The Wallflowers</a> played at the Apple campus.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>59<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Apple_music_event_(September_7,_2005)"><span id="Apple_music_event_.28September_7.2C_2005.29"></span>Apple music event (September 7, 2005)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=54" title="Edit section: Apple music event (September 7, 2005)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>The event was held at the Moscone Convention Center in San Francisco.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-time2005sep&#95;60-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-time2005sep-60"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>60<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Steve Jobs announced:
</p>
<ul><li>The <a href="/wiki/IPod_Nano" title="IPod Nano">iPod Nano</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-AllAboutSJSep2005&#95;61-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AllAboutSJSep2005-61"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>61<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>62<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup></li>
<li>Details about the Motorola ROKR iTunes phone.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-AllAboutSJSep2005&#95;61-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AllAboutSJSep2005-61"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>61<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup></li>
<li>iTunes 5<sup id="cite&#95;ref-AllAboutSJSep2005&#95;61-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AllAboutSJSep2005-61"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>61<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup></li></ul>
<p>Jobs also announced that the <a href="/wiki/Harry_Potter" title="Harry Potter">Harry Potter</a> series of books would be available on the iTunes store as audiobooks.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>63<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Kanye_West" title="Kanye West">Kanye West</a> gave a performance.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-time2005sep&#95;60-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-time2005sep-60"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>60<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-AllAboutSJSep2005&#95;61-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AllAboutSJSep2005-61"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>61<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>64<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> His performance was given after the product announcements.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>65<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> He performed <a href="/wiki/Gold_Digger_(Kanye_West_song)" title="Gold Digger (Kanye West song)">Gold Digger</a> and one other song.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>66<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> The performance was later removed from Apple's online Quicktime stream.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>67<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Apple_special_event_(October_2005)"><span id="Apple_special_event_.28October_2005.29"></span>Apple special event (October 2005)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=55" title="Edit section: Apple special event (October 2005)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Steve Jobs announced:<sup id="cite&#95;ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>68<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li>A new <a href="/wiki/IMac_G5" title="IMac G5">iMac G5</a> with a front-facing camera built in.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Photo_Booth" title="Photo Booth">Photo Booth</a></li>
<li>The iPod Video (also known as the fifth-generation iPod)</li></ul>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="2006">2006</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=56" title="Edit section: 2006"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Macworld_2006_(January_10,_2006)"><span id="Macworld_2006_.28January_10.2C_2006.29"></span>Macworld 2006 (January 10, 2006)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=57" title="Edit section: Macworld 2006 (January 10, 2006)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Apple introduced its first Intel-based computers, the <a href="/wiki/IMac_(Intel-based)" title="IMac (Intel-based)">iMac</a> and <a href="/wiki/MacBook_Pro_(Intel-based)" title="MacBook Pro (Intel-based)">MacBook Pro</a>. Apple also announced new versions of iLife and iWork: iLife '06 and iWork '06. Apple released the first <a href="/wiki/Universal_binary" title="Universal binary">universal binary</a> version of Mac OS X on the same day (version 10.4.4).<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Macworld&#95;06&#95;Computerworld&#95;69-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Macworld_06_Computerworld-69"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>69<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Apple_special_event_(February_28,_2006)"><span id="Apple_special_event_.28February_28.2C_2006.29"></span>Apple special event (February 28, 2006)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=58" title="Edit section: Apple special event (February 28, 2006)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>At the Apple Special Event on February 28, 2006, Steve Jobs announced the <a href="/wiki/IPod_Hi-Fi" title="IPod Hi-Fi">iPod Hi-Fi</a> speaker system at the Town Hall conference center in <a href="/wiki/Cupertino" class="mw-redirect" title="Cupertino">Cupertino</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>70<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="WWDC_2006_(August_7–11,_2006)"><span id="WWDC_2006_.28August_7.E2.80.9311.2C_2006.29"></span>WWDC 2006 (August 7–11, 2006)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=59" title="Edit section: WWDC 2006 (August 7–11, 2006)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>In 2006, Steve Jobs once again delivered the keynote presentation at the WWDC, which was held from August 7 to August 11 in Moscone Center West, San Francisco. The <a href="/wiki/Mac_Pro" title="Mac Pro">Mac Pro</a> was announced as a replacement for the <a href="/wiki/Power_Mac_G5" title="Power Mac G5">Power Mac G5</a>, which was Apple's prior pro desktop computer and the last remaining <a href="/wiki/PowerPC" title="PowerPC">PowerPC</a>-based Mac. The standard Mac Pro featured two 2.66&#160;GHz dual core Xeon (Woodcrest) processors, 1 GB RAM, 250 GB hard drive, and a 256 MB video card. An Xserve update, based on the dual core Xeons, was also announced. Redundant power and Lights Out Management were further product improvements to Apple's server lineup. While certain key <a href="/wiki/MacOS" title="MacOS">Mac OS X</a> improvements were undisclosed, there were 10 improvements in the next iteration, <a href="/wiki/Mac_OS_X_Leopard" title="Mac OS X Leopard">Mac OS X Leopard</a> (10.5), including: full 64-bit app support, <a href="/wiki/Time_Machine_(software)" class="mw-redirect" title="Time Machine (software)">Time Machine</a>, <a href="/wiki/Boot_Camp_(software)" title="Boot Camp (software)">Boot Camp</a>, <a href="/wiki/Front_Row_(software)" title="Front Row (software)">Front Row</a>, <a href="/wiki/Photo_Booth" title="Photo Booth">Photo Booth</a>, Spaces (Virtual Desktops), <a href="/wiki/Spotlight_(software)" class="mw-redirect" title="Spotlight (software)">Spotlight</a> enhancements, Core Animation, Universal Access enhancements, <a href="/wiki/Mail_(OS_X)" class="mw-redirect" title="Mail (OS X)">Mail</a> enhancements, and <a href="/wiki/Dashboard_(Mac_OS_X)" class="mw-redirect" title="Dashboard (Mac OS X)">Dashboard</a> enhancements (including <a href="/wiki/Dashcode" title="Dashcode">Dashcode</a>), and <a href="/wiki/IChat" title="IChat">iChat</a> enhancements. Along with the Leopard features that were announced, a major revision to the Mac OS X Server product was announced. New features to the Server included: a simplified set-up process, <a href="/wiki/ICal_Server" class="mw-redirect" title="ICal Server">iCal Server</a> (based on the <a href="/wiki/CalDAV" title="CalDAV">CalDAV</a> standard), <a href="/wiki/Apple_Teams" class="mw-redirect" title="Apple Teams">Apple Teams</a> (a set of web-based collaborative services), Spotlight Server, and Podcast Producer. The 2006 WWDC attracted 4,200 developers from 48 countries, while there were 140 sessions and 100 hands-on labs for developers. More than 1,000 Apple engineers were present at the event, and the DJ <a href="/wiki/BT_(musician)" title="BT (musician)">BT</a> performed at the Apple Campus in Cupertino.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>71<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>72<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Apple_special_event_(September_12,_2006)"><span id="Apple_special_event_.28September_12.2C_2006.29"></span>Apple special event (September 12, 2006)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=60" title="Edit section: Apple special event (September 12, 2006)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>The event was titled, "It's Showtime". Apple announced the <a href="/wiki/Apple_TV_(device)" title="Apple TV (device)">Apple TV</a> (code-named iTV at the time), along with refreshes to the iPod line, including the 2nd generation iPod nano and a revised iPod Shuffle.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>73<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>74<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="2007">2007</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=61" title="Edit section: 2007"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Macworld_Conference_&amp;_Expo_San_Francisco_2007_(January_9,_2007)"><span id="Macworld_Conference_.26_Expo_San_Francisco_2007_.28January_9.2C_2007.29"></span>Macworld Conference &amp; Expo San Francisco 2007 (January 9, 2007)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=62" title="Edit section: Macworld Conference &amp; Expo San Francisco 2007 (January 9, 2007)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>At Moscone West in San Francisco, Apple introduced the iPhone, with the release date set later in 2007. Besides Jobs, the keynote featured executives from Apple (<a href="/wiki/Phil_Schiller" title="Phil Schiller">Phil Schiller</a>), <a href="/wiki/Google" title="Google">Google</a> (<a href="/wiki/Eric_Schmidt" title="Eric Schmidt">Eric Schmidt</a>), <a href="/wiki/Yahoo" title="Yahoo">Yahoo</a> (<a href="/wiki/Jerry_Yang" title="Jerry Yang">Jerry Yang</a>), and <a href="/wiki/Cingular" class="mw-redirect" title="Cingular">Cingular</a> (<a href="/wiki/Stan_Sigman" title="Stan Sigman">Stan Sigman</a>).
</p><p>Notable Information:
</p>
<ul><li>One year after the announcement of the transition from PowerPC to Intel processors Jobs calls the transition "the smoothest and most successful transition that we've ever seen in the history of our industry".</li>
<li>Jobs announces that over half of all new Mac sales are to "switchers" - users who have never before owned a Mac.</li>
<li>Ad: "I'm a Mac, I'm a PC" ad about Windows upgrading to Vista.</li>
<li>Jobs announces the iPod has become "the world's most popular video player" and the iPod nano is "the world's most popular MP3 player" and the iPod shuffle is "the world's most wearable MP3 player".</li>
<li>2 billion (cumulative) songs purchased and downloaded from iTunes.</li>
<li>5 million songs purchased and downloaded every day from iTunes.</li>
<li>Apple becomes the 4th largest retailer of music behind Walmart, Best Buy, and Target - pulling ahead of Amazon.</li>
<li>Over 350 TV shows now offered on iTunes with 50 million TV shows purchased and downloaded. 1.3 million movies purchased and downloaded.</li>
<li>Jobs announces Paramount will join as a partner to sell movies on iTunes. A total of 250 movies now offered on iTunes.</li>
<li>Jobs announces that as of November 2006 NPD reports iPod has 62% share of portable music device market share.</li>
<li>Ads: 2 versions of iPod "silhouette" commercial featuring <i>Flathead</i> by The Fratellis.</li>
<li>Update on Apple TV (formerly iTV) with demo. Priced at $299 and set to ship the following month ("February"). Orders available following the keynote.</li>
<li>Introduction of the iPhone, "a revolutionary product that changes everything". "Today Apple is going to reinvent the phone."</li>
<li>Introduction of "Multi-Touch", a patented user interface used on the iPhone.</li>
<li>First iPhone 4 GB model to launch at $499 with a 2-year contract. 8 GB model to launch at $599 with a 2-year contract.</li>
<li>First iPhones to ship in the United States in June.</li>
<li>Apple partnering with Cingular as an exclusive partner for the iPhone.</li>
<li>Renaming of "Apple Computer, Inc" to "Apple Inc." to recognize the shift from being a computer company to a broader technology company.</li></ul>
<p>The following day, Apple shares hit $97.80, an all-time high at that point. In May, Apple's share price passed the $100 mark.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>75<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="WWDC_2007_(June_11–15,_2007)"><span id="WWDC_2007_.28June_11.E2.80.9315.2C_2007.29"></span>WWDC 2007 (June 11–15, 2007)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=63" title="Edit section: WWDC 2007 (June 11–15, 2007)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1305433154" /><table class="box-Unreferenced&#95;section plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Unreferenced" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="icon" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/60px-Question_book-new.svg.png" decoding="async" width="50" height="39" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/120px-Question_book-new.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="399" /></a></span></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section <b>does not <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources">cite</a> any <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">sources</a></b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help <a href="/wiki/Special:EditPage/List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events" title="Special:EditPage/List of Apple Inc. media events">improve this section</a> by <a href="/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners" title="Help:Referencing for beginners">adding citations to reliable sources</a>. Unsourced material may be challenged and <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Burden_of_evidence" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">removed</a>.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">June 2024</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>During the event, Apple announced release dates for the first-generation <a href="/wiki/IPhone_(1st_generation)" title="IPhone (1st generation)">iPhone</a> running <a href="/wiki/IPhone_OS_1" title="IPhone OS 1">iPhone OS</a> (rebranded as <a href="/wiki/IOS" title="IOS">iOS</a> in June 2010).
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Apple_special_event_(August_7,_2007)"><span id="Apple_special_event_.28August_7.2C_2007.29"></span>Apple special event (August 7, 2007)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=64" title="Edit section: Apple special event (August 7, 2007)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Apple presented the first iMacs made from aluminum instead of plastic.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>76<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>77<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>78<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> The Mac Mini was also refreshed.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>79<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> iLife '08 and iWork '08 were also announced.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>80<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>81<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Apple_music_event_(September_5,_2007)"><span id="Apple_music_event_.28September_5.2C_2007.29"></span>Apple music event (September 5, 2007)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=65" title="Edit section: Apple music event (September 5, 2007)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>The event was called "The Beat Goes On". Apple announced a new lineup of iPods, including the first generation iPod touch and third generation iPod nano.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>82<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Custom ringtones, the discontinuation of the 4 GB model, and a price drop from $599 to $399 for the 8 GB model were also announced for the iPhone.
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="2008">2008</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=66" title="Edit section: 2008"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Macworld_Conference_&amp;_Expo_San_Francisco_2008_(January_16,_2008)"><span id="Macworld_Conference_.26_Expo_San_Francisco_2008_.28January_16.2C_2008.29"></span>Macworld Conference &amp; Expo San Francisco 2008 (January 16, 2008)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=67" title="Edit section: Macworld Conference &amp; Expo San Francisco 2008 (January 16, 2008)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>On January 16, 2008, at Moscone West in San Francisco, Jobs held a keynote where Apple’s first <a href="/wiki/Ultra-portable" class="mw-redirect" title="Ultra-portable">ultra-portable</a> notebook, <a href="/wiki/MacBook_Air" title="MacBook Air">MacBook Air</a>, was introduced.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>83<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> In addition to that, the iTunes Store launched movie rentals available to play on computers and the Apple TV. Jim Gianopulos, the Chairman and CEO of <a href="/wiki/20th_Century_Fox" class="mw-redirect" title="20th Century Fox">20th Century Fox</a>, and Paul Otellini, the CEO of <a href="/wiki/Intel" title="Intel">Intel</a>, participated in the keynote as invited co-hosts.
</p><p>Notable Information:
</p>
<ul><li>Microsoft has begun shipping Microsoft Office for Mac 2008.</li>
<li>Jobs introduced Time Capsule, a wireless Time Machine back-up appliance based on and including AirPort Extreme.</li>
<li>Jobs reports 4 million iPhones have sold in the 200 days they've been shipping.</li>
<li>Gartner report for Q3 2007 shows iPhone #2 (19.5%) in US SmartPhone Marketshare, behind RIM (Blackberry) (39.0%).</li>
<li>Jobs mentions the Software Developer Kit (SDK) for iPhone.</li>
<li>Jobs spoke to new features in iPhone software release that was released day-of. iPod Touch to get new apps and software update for $20.</li>
<li>Jobs introduced iTunes movie rentals. Launching with over 1000 movies. New movies available 30 days after DVD release. Rentals between $2.99 and $3.99.</li>
<li>Jobs introduced an updated Apple TV "Take 2", a free software upgrade, that no longer required a computer. Price dropped from $299 to $229.</li>
<li>Jobs introduced the MacBook Air - "the world's thinnest notebook". MacBook Air launched with an 80 GB HDD standard or an optional 64 GB SSD. MacBook Air will launch at $1799. Shipping starts 2 weeks from the keynote.</li>
<li>Ad: MacBook Air "envelope" ad featuring <i>New Soul</i> by Yael Naïm.</li></ul>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="iPhone_Software_Roadmap_event_(March_6,_2008)"><span id="iPhone_Software_Roadmap_event_.28March_6.2C_2008.29"></span>iPhone Software Roadmap event (March 6, 2008)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=68" title="Edit section: iPhone Software Roadmap event (March 6, 2008)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Apple announced push technology in iPhone OS, the iPhone SDK and iPhoneOS 2.0.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>84<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="WWDC_2008_(June_9–13,_2008)"><span id="WWDC_2008_.28June_9.E2.80.9313.2C_2008.29"></span>WWDC 2008 (June 9–13, 2008)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=69" title="Edit section: WWDC 2008 (June 9–13, 2008)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Announcements at the keynote included the <a href="/wiki/App_Store_(iOS)" class="mw-redirect" title="App Store (iOS)">App Store</a> for iPhone and <a href="/wiki/IPod_Touch" title="IPod Touch">iPod Touch</a>, the stable version of the <a href="/wiki/IPhone_SDK" class="mw-redirect" title="IPhone SDK">iPhone SDK</a>, announced in March 2008, a subsidized <a href="/wiki/3G" title="3G">3G</a> <a href="/wiki/IPhone_3G" title="IPhone 3G">version</a> of the iPhone for worldwide markets, <a href="/wiki/IOS_version_history" title="IOS version history">version 2.0</a> of <a href="/wiki/IOS" title="IOS">iPhone OS</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mac_OS_X_Snow_Leopard" title="Mac OS X Snow Leopard">Mac OS X Snow Leopard</a> (10.6), and the replacement/rebranding of .Mac as <a href="/wiki/MobileMe" title="MobileMe">MobileMe</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>85<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Apple_special_event_(September_9,_2008)"><span id="Apple_special_event_.28September_9.2C_2008.29"></span>Apple special event (September 9, 2008)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=70" title="Edit section: Apple special event (September 9, 2008)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Updates on iTunes and music from Jobs, retirement of the larger form-factor iPod Classic with hard drive capacity increased on the remaining iPod Classic, and a new iPod Nano. At this event Jobs also joked "the reports of my death are greatly exaggerated".<sup id="cite&#95;ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>86<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Apple_special_event_(October_14,_2008)"><span id="Apple_special_event_.28October_14.2C_2008.29"></span>Apple special event (October 14, 2008)</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=List_of_Apple_Inc._media_events&amp;action=edit&amp;section=71" title="Edit section: Apple special event (October 14, 2008)"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Unibody_Macbook_Pro" class="mw-redirect" title="Unibody Macbook Pro">Unibody Macbook Pro</a> was announced.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>87<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
